# Mozzarella

<section class="hmi-quick-read" id="mozzarella-quick-read" data-hmi-quick-read aria-labelledby="mozzarella-quick-read-label"><div class="hmi-quick-read-label" id="mozzarella-quick-read-label">Quick read</div>

Mozzarella in the HMI library has 66 reported results for lead, cadmium, total arsenic, inorganic arsenic, dimethylarsinic acid, total mercury, nickel, chromium, aluminium, tin, uranium, Co, Cu, Fe, Mn, Zn, V, Se, Ba, Hg, Sr and Ti from 5 sources.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span Marcus et al. 2026 reported lead at 8 µg/kg (mean) in mozzarella, as sold from Buffalo mozzarella (farm-origin, Certeze area).<sup class="cite">[[sources/marcus2026-organic-conventional-dairy-icpms|4]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Marcus OI, Tabaran A, Crisan Reget OL, Dan SD, Panait LC, Lacatus CM, Popescu M, Codea AR, Purdoiu RC, Lacatus R, Petrescu-Mag IV, Nicolescu A, Bora FD · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/marcus2026-organic-conventional-dairy-icpms">From Farm to Retail: Decoding the Elemental Landscape of Milk and Dairy Products Across Organic and Conventional Production Systems Using ICP-MS</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Toxics 2026, 14, 124</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/marcus2026-organic-conventional-dairy-icpms">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> Crupi et al. 2022 reported cadmium at 23 µg/kg (mean ± 3 SD) in mozzarella, as sold from retail, markets in Sicily.<sup class="cite">[[sources/crupi2022-sicily-lactose-free-dairy-elements|5]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Crupi R, Lo Turco V, Gugliandolo E, Nava V, Potortì AG, Cuzzocrea S, Di Bella G, Licata P · 2022</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/crupi2022-sicily-lactose-free-dairy-elements">Mineral Composition in Delactosed Dairy Products: Quality and Safety Status</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Foods 2022, 11, 139</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/crupi2022-sicily-lactose-free-dairy-elements">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

For a buyer the practical points are the form bought, the basis a result is reported on, where the mozzarella came from and the laboratory’s reporting limit. Testing finds contamination but does not remove it.

<div class="hmi-analyte-explorer" id="hmi-occurrence-mozzarella" data-analyte-explorer data-compact-intro="true" data-source-summaries="true"><details class="hmi-drawer-disclosure" data-explorer-context><summary data-open-context aria-haspopup="dialog" aria-controls="hmi-occurrence-mozzarella-drawer">Comparison methods and scope</summary><div data-explorer-context-content>

<p>Each row is one statistic a source reports for one form of mozzarella, group and metal; percentiles, ranges and maxima reported for the same samples as a mean or median are shown on that row. Different forms and preparation states are kept apart, and dry-weight results never share a reference with as-sold or fresh-weight results. Ratings place a result against an HMI reference built from monitoring results and ordinary-setting medians for the same form and basis. Same-study contrasts compare a contaminated or treated site with its control, targeted with random sampling, a prepared form with the unprocessed one, or one group of a study with the median of its other groups. Held rows failed a check against their own source and are shown but not compared.</p>

Mozzarella, as sold; As sold, as eaten or fresh weight, µg/kg; no moisture conversion. Distribution of subgroup median concentration values.</p><div class="hmi-drawer-table"><table><thead><tr><th scope="col">Reference (µg/kg)</th><th scope="col">Q1</th><th scope="col">Median</th><th scope="col">Q3</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td>Primary HMI reference</td><td>≤3.5</td><td>3.5–4 (bounded)</td><td>5.25–6.25 (bounded)</td></tr><tr><td>Alternative weighting</td><td>≤3.5</td><td>3.5–4 (bounded)</td><td>5.25–6.25 (bounded)</td></tr></tbody></table></div><p>30 reference units; 3 sampling families. Counts describe evidence units, not independent studies or certainty.</p><div class="hmi-drawer-table"><table><thead><tr><th scope="col">Contributing family</th><th scope="col">Reference units</th><th scope="col">Weight</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td>WHO GEMS/Food</td><td>2</td><td>33%</td></tr><tr><td>FDA Total Diet Study</td><td>27</td><td>33%</td></tr><tr><td>Anguebes-Franseschi et al. 2023</td><td>1</td><td>33%</td></tr></tbody></table></div><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Methods</p><p>HMI pools subgroup medians for mozzarella, as sold on a as sold, as eaten or fresh weight basis: monitoring results summarized as one median per reporting region, sampling type and collection year, total diet composites and printed sample values as single observations, and printed subgroup medians from ordinary farms or markets. Each sampling family has equal total weight. Non-detects stay as zero-to-limit intervals, so quartiles carry lower and upper bounds. Means, ranges and maxima never enter as observations. Other forms, other bases, targeted sampling and results from mining, industrial, wastewater-affected, experimental, fertiliser or manufacturer settings are kept out of this reference.</p><p>Inverse weighted empirical CDF; midpoint at exact cumulative-weight boundaries. At least four reference units and a definitely positive Q1 to Q3 spread are required. This is a display eligibility rule, not statistical validation.</p><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Limitations</p><p>3 sampling families with different designs; none is a probability sample of world supply. Reporting regions and purchase markets are not growing areas. Non-detects with differing limits widen the bounds. A position is descriptive concentration, not a safety or compliance judgment.</p><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Admitted source families</p><ul><li><a href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants" class="internal" data-slug="sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants" data-no-cite-meta>WHO GEMS/Food</a></li><li><a href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020" class="internal" data-slug="sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020" data-no-cite-meta>FDA Total Diet Study</a></li><li><a href="../sources/anguebesfranseschi2023-mexico-artisanal-cheese-metals" class="internal" data-slug="sources/anguebesfranseschi2023-mexico-artisanal-cheese-metals" data-no-cite-meta>Anguebes-Franseschi et al. 2023</a></li></ul><details class="hmi-drawer-disclosure"><summary>Reference version and interpretation</summary><p>Frozen evidence selection: 2026-10-22. Different HMI scopes are not one common ranking.</p></details></div></details><div hidden data-form-reasons><p data-reason="no_numeric_value_or_bound" data-title="Spread or extreme value only">The source reports only a percentile, range or maximum for these samples, with no mean or median to place against the reference. Spread figures that do have a mean or median are shown on that row instead.</p><p data-reason="bounds_span_categories" data-title="Non-detect spans the bands">The result is below a reporting limit, and the range from zero to that limit crosses more than one band.</p><p data-reason="species_or_material_without_compatible_reference_after_full_ledger" data-title="No reference for this form">No medians or sample values exist for this form and metal, so no HMI reference could be built.</p><p data-reason="reference_below_display_minimum_after_full_ledger" data-title="Reference too small">The HMI reference for this form has fewer than four units or no measurable spread, below the display minimum.</p><p data-reason="basis_incompatible_with_all_references" data-title="Basis has no reference">The result is on a basis (dry weight, or not stated) that no HMI reference for this form uses, so it cannot be placed.</p><p data-reason="bounded" data-title="Limits overlap">Reporting limits overlap the study comparison, so no direction can be shown.</p></div><script type="application/octet-stream" data-explorer-payload <a id="sources-and-contamination-pathways"></a>
## How metals reach mozzarella

<a id="hmi-ing-010"></a>
<a id="what-evidence-supports-root-uptake-surface-deposition-feed-transfer-or-other-biological-pathways"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-011"></a>
<a id="what-contamination-can-arise-from-agricultural-inputs-harvesting-processing-equipment-or-packaging"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-012"></a>
<a id="which-source-attribution-claims-are-measured-modelled-inferred-or-unresolved"></a>

No study in the library compared mozzarella from contaminated and ordinary settings, so the routes by which metals reach it are not measured here.



<a id="ranges-by-source-region-and-variety"></a>
## Which forms and regions measured lower

<a id="hmi-ing-013"></a>
<a id="how-do-concentrations-vary-by-origin-growing-conditions-variety-and-harvest-period"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-014"></a>
<a id="what-evidence-distinguishes-within-lot-between-lot-supplier-and-production-system-variation"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-015"></a>
<a id="which-comparisons-support-a-sourcing-decision-and-where-would-geographic-or-supplier-generalization-be-unjustified"></a>

The HMI reference for lead in mozzarella, as sold pools 30 subgroup units from 3 sampling families, with a median of between 3.5 and 4 µg/kg and a middle half spanning below 3.5 (lower quartile) and between 5.25 and 6.25 µg/kg (upper quartile).<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span <a id="mitigation-options"></a>
## Mitigation for mozzarella

<a id="hmi-ing-019"></a>
<a id="which-prevention-mitigation-or-remediation-measures-have-been-evaluated-for-this-ingredient"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-020"></a>
<a id="under-what-conditions-at-which-stage-and-against-which-comparator-do-they-work-or-fail"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-021"></a>
<a id="what-evidence-supports-field-or-commercial-implementation-and-ongoing-verification"></a>

Lot testing against the applicable limit is the check that can be verified.

<p class="hmi-mitigation-gap" data-mitigation-gap>No measured way to lower metals in this food is in the library yet.</p>



<a id="trade-offs-and-failure-modes"></a>
## Trade-offs and limits of controls

<a id="hmi-ing-022"></a>
<a id="can-an-intervention-increase-another-contaminant-or-redistribute-contamination-to-a-by-product"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-023"></a>
<a id="what-effects-on-nutrition-yield-sensory-quality-stability-and-waste-streams-were-measured"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-024"></a>
<a id="what-costs-constraints-adverse-effects-or-failure-modes-are-documented-and-which-are-unknown"></a>

Metals do not move together, so a lot that passes a lead limit says little about cadmium, arsenic or nickel unless those are tested as well.



<a id="sampling-and-testing"></a>
## Testing mozzarella for metals

<a id="hmi-ing-025"></a>
<a id="what-sampling-design-is-appropriate-for-the-ingredient-heterogeneity-lot-and-decision"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-026"></a>
<a id="which-methods-species-measurements-validation-evidence-and-reporting-limits-are-needed"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-027"></a>
<a id="what-can-a-certificate-of-analysis-or-non-detect-establish-and-what-remains-untested"></a>

Results depend on the method and its reporting limit. Reporting limits for lead ranged from 4 µg/kg (FDA Total Diet Study) to 5 µg/kg (WHO GEMS/Food), so a non-detect from a less sensitive method says much less.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a <a id="universal-faqs"></a>
## Mozzarella FAQs

<details class="qa" data-hmi-faq="1" id="what-heavy-metals-are-found-in-mozzarella" data-anchor="what-heavy-metals-are-found-in-mozzarella" data-question="What heavy metals are found in mozzarella?">
<summary><span class="mk" aria-hidden="true"></span>What heavy metals are found in mozzarella?</summary>
<div class="ans" data-hmi-faq-answer="1">

The library reports lead, cadmium, total arsenic, inorganic arsenic, dimethylarsinic acid, total mercury, nickel, chromium, aluminium, tin, uranium, Co, Cu, Fe, Mn, Zn, V, Se, Ba, Hg, Sr and Ti in mozzarella, most often lead, cadmium and total arsenic.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span <details class="qa" data-hmi-faq="1" id="how-much-of-the-arsenic-in-mozzarella-is-inorganic" data-anchor="how-much-of-the-arsenic-in-mozzarella-is-inorganic" data-question="How much of the arsenic in mozzarella is inorganic?">
<summary><span class="mk" aria-hidden="true"></span>How much of the arsenic in mozzarella is inorganic?</summary>
<div class="ans" data-hmi-faq-answer="1">

Inorganic arsenic, the more toxic form, was measured in 2 results from 1 source, far fewer than total arsenic.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> WHO GEMS/Food submissions reported inorganic arsenic in mozzarella, as sold from 3 results in 1 reporting region, with subgroup medians below 40 µg/kg, mostly non-detects.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

</div>
</details>
